Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Well-separated temperature mixing (T=0.7+T=1.0) achieves Pareto optimality for both uniform and VC construction, while VC-weighted construction flattens the trade-off slope, reducing the diversity cost of quality.\", \"metrics\": {\"dataset\": \"SciERC\", \"configs\": {\"t07\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.777, \"uniform_delta\": 1.81, \"vc_delta\": 1.17, \"pareto\": \"uniform only\"}, \"t08\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.819, \"uniform_delta\": 1.25, \"vc_delta\": 1.68, \"pareto\": \"VC only\"}, \"mix_07_10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.83, \"uniform_delta\": 1.43, \"vc_delta\": 1.67, \"pareto\": \"both\"}, \"t10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.877, \"uniform_delta\": 0.75, \"vc_delta\": 1.21, \"pareto\": \"both (highest diversity)\"}, \"mix_08_10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.789, \"uniform_delta\": -0.17, \"vc_delta\": 0.47, \"pareto\": \"dominated\"}}, \"vc_slope_reduction\": \"~50% flatter than uniform\", \"key_insight\": \"well-separated T mixing dual Pareto optimal; close T mixing dominated\"}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"A4 Pareto diversity-quality analysis on SciERC. Mix T=0.7+T=1.0 is dual-Pareto optimal (uniform +1.43pp, VC +1.67pp). VC construction flattens trade-off slope ~50% vs uniform — increasing diversity costs less quality. Mix T=0.8+T=1.0 is dominated (close T → higher degeneracy, no construction gain). Confirms DGEC heterogeneous-T finding: ΔT matters, ΔT=0.3 beneficial, ΔT=0.2 harmful. Pareto plot artifact registered.\"}, \"exp_adaptive_sampling_budget\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:29:22.761Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:29:41.158Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Confidence-aware adaptive sampling budget allocation (fewer samples for high-confidence instances, full N=8 for low-confidence) can recover nearly all SC F1 gains while saving 40-50% of inference cost, because high-confidence instances already have low diversity and gain nothing from additional samples.\", \"metrics\": {\"model\": \"Qwen3-8B FT\", \"fewnerd\": {\"n_instances\": 37648, \"greedy_f1\": 0.7884, \"uniform_n8_f1\": 0.7819, \"best_adaptive\": \"h1_l8\", \"best_adaptive_f1\": 0.7849, \"best_delta_vs_u8\": \"+0.30pp\", \"sample_savings\": \"43.8%\", \"note\": \"Greedy > U8; adaptive h1/l8 best: greedy for high-conf, N=8 SC for low-conf\"}, \"scierc\": {\"n_instances\": 551, \"greedy_f1\": 0.6592, \"uniform_n8_f1\": 0.6712, \"best_adaptive\": \"top67%_h2_l8\", \"best_adaptive_f1\": 0.6707, \"best_delta_vs_u8\": \"-0.05pp\", \"sample_savings\": \"50.2%\", \"note\": \"U8 > Greedy; top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with negligible loss\"}}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"B7 adaptive sampling budget analysis on Qwen3-8B FT. Key findings: (1) FewNERD: greedy already beats uniform N=8 SC (+0.65pp), adaptive h1/l8 is optimal (+0.30pp vs U8, saves 43.8% samples); (2) SciERC: U8 beats greedy (+1.20pp), top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with only -0.05pp loss. Practical implication: confidence-gated sampling can halve inference cost.
